{"parentUuid":"bcda8eb7-a977-4f4c-bed7-1a7a6884f37e","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a09f20522ff9ad768","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdAtzRKDwXJ4eYRdtjJuW","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01UD9CZxoYc9F4vnS9qA9W7N","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cat > /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/repro_dedup2.py <<'PY'\n\"\"\"Realistic approval dedup (tgt = a real existing queued seq) vs the out-of-range-tgt BYPASS.\"\"\"\nimport sys, os, time\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(__file__))\nfrom _h import store, authed_device, OP\nfrom sigil.bridge.daemon import BridgeDaemon\nfrom sigil.reuse import sign\nfrom sigil.agents.approvals import _approval_message, SIGNAL as APPROVAL_SIGNAL\n\ndef approval(dev, tgt):\n    dec, app = \"approved\", \"owner-phone\"\n    msg = _approval_message(tgt, dec, app)\n    return {\"signal\":APPROVAL_SIGNAL,\"approval\":dec,\"target_seq\":tgt,\"approver\":app,\n            \"pubkey\":dev.public_key_b64,\"sig\":sign(dev.private_key_b64,msg)}\n\ns = store(); dev,_ = authed_device(s)\nd = BridgeDaemon(s, trusted_pubkey=OP)\n# queue a REAL item first, approve its actual seq (the honest path: tgt < tip)\nreal_tgt = s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"gesture\", actor=\"PERCEPTION\",\n                    payload={\"signal\":\"gesture.action\",\"decision\":\"queued\",\"status\":\"awaiting-approval\",\"tier\":\"A2\"})\nap = approval(dev, real_tgt)\na1=d.submit_device_approval(ap); a2=d.submit_device_approval(ap); a3=d.submit_device_approval(ap)\nprint(f\"approval dedup REALISTIC tgt={real_tgt} (exists, < tip): a1={a1} a2={a2} a3={a3} -> {'DEDUP OK' if a1==a2==a3 else 'BLOATS'}\")\n# out-of-range tgt bypass\nap2 = approval(dev, 10**18)\nbefore=s.count(); Now I have everything to render the verdict.\n\n---\n\n# RED-PEN RE-CHECK — SIGIL Phase 9 \"COMPANION\" (branch `phase9-companion` @ `57525d5`)\n\n## Verdict: BLOCK (1 MED live-crash) + 1 MED latency regression + 2 LOW. All 7 original exploits are DEAD.\n\nI reconstructed the repros from scratch (the scratchpad had none), re-ran every original exploit against the fixed code, and stress-hunted the new locks. The seven prior findings are genuinely fixed. But the BLOCK-1 nonce hardening is **incomplete** and re-opens the exact robustness class the prior review BLOCKed on (BLOCK-3), reachable live.\n\n---\n\n## Part A — the 7 original fixes: STATUS per item\n\n**1. BLOCK-1 NaN/non-finite arm — HELD.** `repro_arm_edges.py`: `ts=NaN`, `ttl=NaN`, `ttl=+Inf`, `ts=+Inf`, `ttl=1e400`, `ttl=-5`, `ttl=0`, `ts=True`, huge-future `ts` → **all refused**. Only legitimate inputs (`ttl=120`, `ttl=\"120\"`, `nonce=1.5`) arm, and the clamp still applies. No freshness/clamp bypass on the arm path. `math.isfinite` guards at `session.py:184` and `:202` are load-bearing.\n\n**2. BLOCK-2 self-verify above the cap — HELD.** `ttl=99999` → record stores `ttl_seconds=99999.0` (original, signed) + `effective_ttl=300.0` (clamped); the live session is bound to 300s; the sig re-verifies against the device ledger. I grep-confirmed the enforcement value is **only** `effective_ttl` — nothing anywhere reads the original `ttl_seconds` as an enforcement input (the clamp is not moot).\n\n**3. BLOCK-3 verify_envelope malformed sig — HELD (incl. live).** `repro_live.py`: malformed short sig on `/api/pending` → clean **HTTP 401** `{\"error\":\"unauthenticated: signature invalid\"}`, not a crash/RemoteDisconnected. Valid read → 200.\n\n**4. BLOCK-4/MED-4 nonce TOCTOU — HELD.** `repro_toctou.py`: 8 concurrent replays of one effectful envelope → **accepted 1, refused 7**.\n\n**5. HIGH-2 spine chain under concurrency — HELD, stressed hard.** `repro_stress.py`: 24 threads × 40 mixed ops (append + consume + submit_arm + submit_approval + concurrent read/verify), fresh `SpineStore` per op like the server → completed in 7.1s (**no hang**, exit 0), 701 records, `verify()` = True, seqs **distinct and contiguous 0..n-1**, zero reader crashes (no partial-line `json` errors).\n\n**6. HIGH-1 kill-switch cache — HELD.** `repro_ks_cache.py`: worst-case injection-after-panic window = **exactly 0.5s** (= `_KS_CACHE_TTL`); after release the cache stays \"engaged\" ≤0.5s then un-blocks (never stuck-engaged); the **arm path checks FRESH** (uncached) so arming-during-panic is refused.\n\n**7. HIGH-3 wiring / attempt-once — HELD.** `repro_wiring.py`: fresh recorded request → arms (1 record); stale request over 8 frames → **exactly 1 refusal** (no per-frame spam), never armed.\n\n---\n\n## Part B — NEW defects the fixes introduced / left open\n\n### BLOCK-1 (NEW, MED) — Infinity nonce crashes the effectful envelope path; live `RemoteDisconnected`\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/envelope.py:111-116` — the BLOCK-1 remediation added `nonce = int(core[\"nonce\"])` guarded by `except (TypeError, ValueError)`. `int(float('nan'))` raises `ValueError` (caught) but **`int(float('inf'))` raises `OverflowError`, which is NOT a subclass of ValueError/TypeError → uncaught.** `canonical_json` serializes `inf` as `Infinity` (reproducible bytes), so an owner-authorized device can validly sign an effectful envelope with `nonce: Infinity`.\n\n- `repro_infinity_nonce.py`: NaN nonce → clean `ValueError(\"invalid nonce\")`; **Inf / -Inf nonce → `>>> UNCAUGHT OverflowError <<<`** escaping `consume()`.\n- `repro_live.py` against the real `BridgeServer`: `POST /api/panic` with a device-signed `nonce=Infinity` → **`RemoteDisconnected: Remote end closed connection without response`**, server thread traceback `envelope.py:112 → OverflowError`. Caller `server.py:188-191` (`_authed_effectful`) wraps `consume` in `except ValueError` only — so the crash escapes to the socketserver, which drops the connection. (Reads are unaffected; only `/api/panic` and `/api/relay` reach `consume`.)\n\nThis is the same class the prior review BLOCKed as BLOCK-3 (\"clean refusal, never a RemoteDisconnected\"), and `envelope.py`'s own docstring claims \"any crypto/decode error is a fail-closed refusal, never a crash\" — contradicted here. Fail-closed for effect (nothing executes; `daemon_threads` keeps the server alive), but it is a live, authorized-device-reachable request-handler crash + stderr traceback. **The test suite hid it:** `test_bridge_envelope.py` tests NaN on the arm path only and has no Infinity-nonce case for the envelope.\n**Fix direction:** add `OverflowError` to the except at `envelope.py:113` (and mirror the arm path's `math.isfinite` pre-check); consider having `_authed_effectful` catch broadly like `_device_action`/`_device_arm` already do.\n\n### FINDING-2 (NEW, MED) — dedup scans run O(spine) *under the process-wide append lock* → latency spike stalls all writers\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/bridge/daemon.py:111-122` (`submit_arm_request` scans the **entire** spine) and `:80-88` (`submit_device_approval` scans `since_seq=(tgt or 0)-1` → O(tip−tgt), i.e. O(spine) for an old target) both hold `spine_lock` across the full scan **and** the append. `repro_latency.py` @ 8,003 records: a plain append = **0.20 ms**, one `submit_arm_request` = **145.94 ms** — and during that 146 ms the global lock blocks *every* other writer (gesture-action receipts, consume receipts, kill-switch appends). Extrapolated to the real ~43k-record spine that is ~0.75 s of stalled input. Pre-fix `append` was O(1) and unlocked, so the concurrency fix introduced this self-inflicted latency/DoS, reachable by any authorized device.\n**Fix direction:** don't hold the append lock across the dedup read — do the dedup scan outside the write lock (accepting the rare benign double-record), or dedup via an index/bounded window rather than a full O(spine) scan.\n\n### FINDING-3 (LOW) — `submit_device_approval` dedup bypass via out-of-range `target_seq`\n`daemon.py:82` — `since_seq=(tgt or 0)-1` assumes the duplicate record's seq ≥ `tgt`. `repro_dedup2.py`: a realistic `target_seq` (a real queued seq < tip) dedups correctly (`a1==a2==a3`); a device-signed approval with `target_seq=10**18` (≥ tip) → the scan starts past the tip, finds nothing, and **every replay re-appends** (`h1=3,h2=4,h3=5`). Honest path fine; a malicious authorized device can bloat. **Fix:** clamp `since_seq` to a safe lower bound (e.g. `min(tgt, tip)-1`, or `-1` for out-of-range/None/negative).\n\n### FINDING-4 (LOW) — remote-arm starvation: a newer stale request shadows an older valid one\n`session.py:48-64` (`pending_device_arm` returns **only the newest** candidate) + `run.py:61,69-71` (attempt-once). `repro_starvation.py`: R1 (valid, fresh, older seq) + R2 (newer seq, future-ts → fails freshness). `pending_device_arm` returns R2; `run_gesture` attempts R2 once (refused), then R2 permanently pins the \"newest\" slot → **R1 is never attempted → never arms** (control: R1 alone arms fine). Fail-closed (a legit arm silently doesn't happen; no security bypass). **Fix:** yield all not-yet-armed candidates (oldest-first) instead of only the newest.\n\n### OBSERVATION (informational, accepted/disclosed) — 0.5 s injection window after a phone panic\nThe HIGH-1 cache means a device-armed session the owner \"may not be watching\" can keep injecting for up to `_KS_CACHE_TTL` = 0.5 s (~15 frames @ 30 fps) after a panic before `handle()` re-checks. This is the deliberate, code-documented tradeoff (\"panic latency is bounded by the TTL\") and the safe direction fails closed — not a regression, but worth the owner's eyes; tighten the TTL if 0.5 s of post-panic pointer input is unacceptable.\n\n---\n\n## Property attestation (what I attacked, and why it held / broke)\n- **Oracle soundness (the sig/freshness/replay/kill-switch/TTL gate chain).** Tried to arm with every non-finite/edge input — all refused (fail-closed); confirmed no forged/`_refuse_arm` record can masquerade as a `pending_device_arm` candidate (it re-verifies the sig and filters `decision=='auto'+sig+pubkey`). **Held.** The only malformed input that \"fires\" is the Infinity-nonce, which fails *closed* (crash, no effect) — a robustness bug (BLOCK above), not a soundness bypass.\n- **No silent promotion.** The armed record's authorization re-verifies against the owner-signed device ledger (BLOCK-2), and `effective_ttl` is the sole enforcement value (grep-confirmed).
